Monday, October 26, 2026

arXiv:2610.32084 §

Prediction of the Geoneutrino Signal at the Jinping Neutrino Experiment

Kaikai Meng et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

We present refined predictions of the geoneutrino flux for the 500-tonne Jinping Neutrino Experiment (JNE). We construct a local crustal model with a resolution of \(0.2^\circ\times0.2^\circ\) based on the SWChinaCVM-2.0 seismic velocity model and integrate it with CRUST1.0 to establish our reference model. For comparison, we also calculate the geoneutrino flux using ECM1 and CRUST1.0 as standalone global crustal models. Assuming an energy scale of 500~p.e./MeV and an exposure of 500~t\(\times\)1500~days, we predict the inverse beta decay event spectrum, including both the geoneutrino signal and the reactor antineutrino background. The distinct spectral shapes of the \(^{238}\)U, \(^{232}\)Th, and reactor antineutrino components allow their respective contributions to be obtained through spectral fitting. These predictions provide inputs for the JNE data analysis, particularly for extracting the mantle geoneutrino signal and testing competing bulk silicate Earth models.

Friday, October 23, 2026

arXiv:2610.30754 §

Natural Chlorine Enhances Antineutrino Detection in the Deep Ocean

Ruike Cao et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Large-volume neutrino telescopes such as IceCube and KM3NeT exploit natural ice or water as detection media, but their sparse instrumentation makes MeV neutrino interactions difficult to identify individually. We show that chlorine naturally abundant in seawater provides an intrinsic neutron-capture target that enables efficient tagging of electron antineutrinos through inverse beta decay. In contrast to the 2.2 MeV \(\gamma\)-ray from neutron capture on hydrogen, capture on chlorine produces an \(\sim\)8.6 MeV \(\gamma\)-ray cascade on a shorter time scale, yielding a distinctive delayed-coincidence signature. Using measured seawater properties at the TRIDENT site and a detector-level simulation, we find that chlorine-assisted tagging can extend the core-collapse supernova 5\(\sigma\) discovery reach from \(\sim\)50 to over 100 kpc. We validate the underlying detection principle with a salted-water experiment at the China Jinping Underground Laboratory, observing correlated neutron-capture signatures under suppressed cosmic muon background conditions. Natural seawater thus transforms the deep ocean into an intrinsically enhanced antineutrino detection medium, opening new opportunities for MeV neutrino astronomy and distributed measurements of reactor and geophysical antineutrinos.

arXiv:2610.30894 §

CURTAIN: Towards a Foundation Model for Neutrino Telescopes

Severin Magel et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Neutrino telescopes record more than a trillion photon detections per year, yet there does not exist a self-supervised learning (SSL) task which fully satisfies all three conditions: improving as the pretraining set grows, transferring to a different detector, and outperforming the best model trained without pretraining at all. We introduce CURTAIN, a temporal masking scheme and the first SSL task for neutrino telescopes that achieves all of these goals. Downstream performance monotonically improves with the number of pretraining events, as predicted by scaling laws. Our encoder builds on DeepIce, currently the leading model on the NuBench benchmarks direction reconstruction task. Pretraining it with CURTAIN reduces its median angular error by 45% and its median energy error by 31% compared to training from scratch. It also transfers to a detector geometry never seen in pretraining, reducing the median angular error by 36% compared to training from scratch. At matched capacity and pretraining data, CURTAIN-pretrained encoders beat those pretrained by existing SSL tasks on both tasks and at every pretraining set size.

arXiv:2610.31291 §

Precise Measurement of the Inclusive Cross Sections of Open-Charm Mesons at Energies from 3.80 to 4.95 GeV

BESIII Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Using \(e^+e^-\) collision data collected by the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies from \(3.80\) to \(4.95~\text{GeV}\), the inclusive production cross sections of open-charm mesons, \(\sigma(e^{+}e^{-}\to D^{0}/D^{+}/D_{s}^{+}+X)\), are measured by tagging a \(D^0\), \(D^+\), or \(D_s^+\) meson, where \(X\) denotes any accompanying particles. Open-charm production dominates the total hadronic yield above threshold. Therefore these measurements provide a vital reference for understanding hadron production and for validating global \(R\) value results. The results offer crucial inputs for investigating heavier charmonia and exotic states coupled to open-charm final states, while serving as a benchmark for comparison with the sum of measured exclusive channels. This comparison directly evaluates the completeness of current exclusive datasets and clarifies the underlying hadronization mechanisms in the open-charm region.

arXiv:2610.31377 §

Measurements of the branching fractions of Λ_c⁺→ΛK⁺ π⁰ and Λ_c⁺→ΛK⁺ η decays at Belle and Belle II

Belle et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

We present a study of the three-body, singly Cabibbo-suppressed decays \(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0\) and \(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta\) using data recorded with the Belle and Belle II detectors at the KEKB and SuperKEKB \(e^+ e^-\) colliders. The data samples correspond to integrated luminosities of 980\(\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}\) and 570\(\,\mathrm{fb}^{-1}\), respectively. We measure their branching-fraction ratios relative to the Cabibbo-favored decay \(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0\) as \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0)/\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)=(1.82 \pm 0.07 \pm 0.07)\%\), with significantly improved precision compared to the previous measurement, and \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta)/\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)=(8.73 \pm 0.35 \pm 0.43)\times10^{-3}\), corresponding to the first observation of the \(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta\) decay with a statistical significance exceeding 10 standard deviations. The first and second uncertainties are statistical and systematic, respectively. Using the world-average value of the branching fraction for \(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0\), we obtain the branching fractions \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \pi^0)=(1.29\pm 0.05\pm 0.05\pm 0.06)\times10^{-3}\) and \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda K^+ \eta)=(6.20\pm 0.25\pm 0.31\pm 0.30)\times10^{-4}\), where the third uncertainty arises from the uncertainty in the world-average value of \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+ \to \Lambda \pi^+ \pi^0)\). The measured \(\mathcal{B}(\Lambda_c^+\to\Lambda K^+\eta)\) is significantly larger than predictions based on \(SU(3)\) flavor symmetry.

arXiv:2610.31430 §

A superconducting LC resonator in a multi-tesla magnetic field for tabletop kHz gravitational-wave detection

D. Ahn et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

We present a concept for a superconducting inductance–capacitance (LC) resonator immersed in a magnetic field for gravitational-wave detection in the kHz band. In the long-wavelength limit, a gravitational wave in a static magnetic field induces an oscillating magnetic-field perturbation proportional to the static magnetic field applied to the resonator and to the gravitational-wave strain. We show that a superconducting pickup circuit can convert this perturbation into a flux signal read out by a superconducting quantum interference device (SQUID)-based or quantum-limited amplifier chain. Inspired by resonant-mass detectors, we develop a three-loop coupled-circuit model and derive the equivalent strain-noise spectrum, including thermal noise, SQUID backaction, and measurement imprecision. Prototype measurements demonstrate a quality factor of \(Q\simeq 5\times 10^5\), while \(Q=10^7\) is considered as a future development target. Under the assumed flux coupling and an idealized quantum-limited SQUID noise model, optimized circuits yield projected strain sensitivities of order \(10^{-21}\,\mathrm{Hz}^{-1/2}\) over \(100\,\mathrm{Hz}\) bands in the \(1\)–\(20\,\mathrm{kHz}\) range. Increasing the active high-field volume and improving quantum-limited readout could enable further sensitivity gains. This compact, tunable detector concept could complement interferometric gravitational-wave searches and motivate investigations of high-frequency astrophysical sources.

Cross Section Measurements and Searches for New Structures in e⁺e⁻ Annihilations at BESIII

Hao Liu

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The BESIII collaboration has collected large data samples in the center-of-mass energy range from 1.84 to 4.95 GeV. These data provide a low-background environment and wide energy range for studying the cross sections and the spectroscopy of charmonia. In this article, we review the recent achievements by the BESIII collaboration, including for searching new structures of \(XYZ\) particles and the hyperonic production over open-charm threshold.

arXiv:2610.29819 §

Study of Λ CP Asymmetry Parameter and Electric Dipole Moment at the Super Tau-Charm Facility

Mingyu Yu et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Searching for new mechanisms of \(CP\) violation beyond the Standard Model is essential to explain the observed matter-antimatter asymmetry of the universe. By analyzing the full angular distribution of the quantum-entangled \(\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}\) pairs from \(J/\psi\) decays, the preliminary sensitivity of \(CP\) asymmetry parameter \(A_{CP}\) and Electric Dipole Moment of \(\Lambda\) is studied at the future Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF). Based on \(3.4 \times 10^{12}\) \(J/\psi\) events at STCF, the projected sensitivity for \(A_{CP}^\Lambda\) reaches the \(10^{-4}\) level, while the \(\Lambda\) EDM can be probed at \(10^{-20}\) \(e\) cm. These results highly indicate that STCF will provide an unprecedented opportunity to search for new mechanisms of \(CP\) violation with an expected world-leading precision.

Wednesday, October 21, 2026

arXiv:2610.27105 §

The Electron-Recoil Response Tail and the LZ High-Energy Event Background

Triparno Bandyopadhyay et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

We reconstruct the continuum electron-recoil contribution to the LZ background expectation from the collaboration’s released simulation configuration. The released model specifies a definite response tail, but the published calibration information supplies no bound on how far the physical tail may depart from it at the probabilities the expectation depends on. Distributions reproducing the executable model’s mean, variance, and standardised skewness exactly, and its event-conditioned 10, 50, and 90\% \(N_e\) quantiles to about one per cent, differ in the relevant tail probability by more than five orders of magnitude. The ladder is short: a tail agreeing with that central characterisation to 0.14\% in the quantiles lifts the electron-recoil contribution out of the negligible range, and one agreeing to 0.47\% makes it comparable to the entire background quoted for the comparison region. We go on to identify the measurements that would settle it.

arXiv:2610.27346 §

Pretraining on boosted boson decays for polarization tagging of hadronic W bosons

Saptaparna Bhattacharya

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Longitudinally polarized weak bosons probe the unitarization of vector-boson scattering. At large boosts, longitudinal and transverse \(W\) bosons produce jets with the same mass and two-prong structure, distinguished only by the decay angle. Longitudinal bosons are rare: they occur in only 0.3% of \(W\)+jet events at these large boosts. We ask where the polarization information of a boosted \(W\) boson lies and how much a tagger can recover. Requiring both decay quarks within the jet truncates the decay-angle distribution at an edge set by \(m/(p_T R)\), which we show bounds any hadronic polarization tagger. Within this acceptance, the decay angle carries essentially all the polarization information of a jet in \(W\)+jet events. Polarized samples are costly to simulate for vector-boson scattering, so we pretrain a point-edge transformer on \(10^7\) unpolarized jets of boosted bosons, top quarks and QCD and test how much it recovers from few labels. Fine-tuned on 783 labeled jets per class with its transformer blocks kept fixed, the network pretrained to classify these jets improves on training from scratch, but generic pretraining on a broad mix of jets and a boosted decision tree on substructure observables do as well. The best taggers recover most, but not all, of the separation the decay angle allows, and student networks with a few hundred times fewer parameters, trained by knowledge distillation, match their teacher. More information lies outside the jet. The production angle, reconstructed with the recoiling jet, improves every tagger. In diboson production and vector-boson scattering at matrix-element level, the production kinematics carry more information than the decay angles of both bosons. Trading the generality of a jet tagger for an event-level tagger trained on the process thus brings significant gains, which measurements of longitudinal vector-boson scattering could exploit.

arXiv:2610.27760 §

Charmed baryon decays at BESIII

Chengzhi He

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

BESIII has accumulated 6.4~fb\(^{-1}\) of \(e^+e^-\) collision data at center-of-mass energies from 4.60 to 4.95~GeV, corresponding to the world’s largest sample of \(\Lambda_c^+\bar\Lambda_c^-\) pairs. This proceeding summarizes recent BESIII results on charmed baryon decays, including the first observation of the transverse polarization of the \(\Lambda_c^+\) in \(e^+e^-\to\Lambda_c^+\bar\Lambda_c^-\), a precision measurement of the inclusive decay \(\Lambda_c^+\to\Lambda X\), the first observations of \(\Lambda_c^+\to ne^+\nu_e\) and \(\Lambda_c^+\to n\pi^+\eta\) with deep-learning techniques, the first observation of \(\Lambda_c^+\to pK^-e^+\nu_e\) together with evidence for \(\Lambda_c^+\to\Sigma^\pm\pi^\mp e^+\nu_e\), and partial wave analyses of \(\Lambda_c^+\to\Lambda\pi^+\eta\).

arXiv:2610.28296 §

Open Science with the LHCb Ntupling Service (Christine A. Aidala

University of Michigan et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The data samples collected at the Large Hadron Collider at CERN are highly valuable, and the analysis of these data has led to numerous discoveries and the 2025 Breakthrough Prize in Fundamental Physics. Preserving and providing open access to these data for future analysts is imperative to maximize the scientific output of the collected data. The LHCb collaboration has developed a novel approach to this pursuit of open science, the LHCb Ntupling Service: a web service for on-demand production and publishing of custom LHCb open data, providing open access to both Run 1, and for the first time, Run 2 proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment, amounting to over 4 PB of data to explore.

arXiv:2610.24472 §

Light meson decays at BESIII

Kailu Li

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The world’s largest sample of \(J/\psi\) events accumulated with the BESIII detector offers a unique opportunity to investigate \(\eta\) and \(\eta'\) physics via the radiative transition processes \(J/\psi \to \gamma\eta/\eta'\). A selection of recent highlights in light meson spectroscopy achieved at BESIII is reviewed in these proceedings, including evidence for the cusp effect in \(\eta' \to \pi^0\pi^0\eta\), precision studies of the decay mechanisms of \(\eta \to 3\pi\) and \(\eta' \to 4\pi\), electromagnetic transition form factor measurements with \(\eta/\eta' \to \gamma e^+e^-\) and \(\eta' \to \pi^+\pi^-l^+l^-\), as well as searches for rare decays of \(\eta\) and \(\eta'\) into lepton pairs, including \(\eta \to l^+l^-\) and \(\eta/\eta' \to l^+l^-l^+l^-\)~(\(l = e, \mu\)).

arXiv:2610.24954 §

ceci n'est pas un tagger, can an LLM flavour-tag a jet?

Andrea Coccaro et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly capable of reasoning about scientific problems, effectively mastering and synthesizing entire fields of research and providing, for the first time, access to a distilled and queryable form of scientific knowledge. As the integration of LLMs rapidly spreads across the large collaborations of high-energy physics, aiming to re-think and optimize analysis workflows, it remains unclear whether the knowledge acquired during pretraining can support quantitative inference on physical data. Moving beyond the applications currently pursued by the community, we pose a simple question: can an LLM classify the flavour of a hadronic jet? An LLM is therefore asked to assign flavour probabilities (b, c, light, \(\tau\)) to jets from a public ATLAS flavour-tagging sample, using only reconstructed constituents. Under a small set of constraints, the model is not allowed to write code or implement conventional algorithms and must instead perform the inference directly from its pretrained knowledge and the available inputs. We report two complementary results and make publicly available, through a GitHub repository, all the code required to reproduce the experiment and obtain the quantitative results presented in this work. First, the model achieves non-trivial discrimination between flavours, which we compare with recent ATLAS flavour-tagging algorithms. Second, the model accompanies each prediction with a natural language rationale. The physical concepts invoked in these rationales correlate with the truth flavour and reproduce the features traditionally associated with each jet category. Flavour tagging thus provides a laboratory for broader questions: how can we actively leverage the corpus of scientific knowledge accumulated over decades of research? Could LLMs serve as a general source of scientific context for task-specific algorithms?

arXiv:2610.25753 §

Search for new physics with charm data at BESIII (Xinyu Chai (on behalf of the BESIII Collaboration))

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Up to now, the Standard Model (SM) remains confronted with several outstanding issues, including the matter-antimatter asymmetry in the universe, as well as the masses, oscillations, and hierarchical structure of neutrinos. These challenges are closely tied to the naturalness and stability of the SM, and have motivated numerous proposals for new physics beyond the SM. The BESIII experiment has accumulated the world’s largest data samples at the \(J/\psi\) and \(\psi(3686)\) resonances, providing an ideal environment for probing signatures of physics beyond the SM. This proceeding presents a summary of recent results published by the BESIII Collaboration on the searches for lepton/baryon number violation (LNV/BNV), charged lepton flavor violation (CLFV), and charmonium weak decays.

arXiv:2610.26148 §

Low-Latency Beam-Background Suppression for the BESIII Trigger System Using Machine Learning Trained on Experimental Data

Hanlin Zhang et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Beam-induced backgrounds contribute to the trigger and data-acquisition rate at high-luminosity electron-positron colliders. Their dependence on beam and machine conditions motivates data-driven studies alongside simulation. A data-driven machine-learning method for trigger-level beam-background suppression is developed using experimental data recorded by BESIII. Compact neural-network classifiers are trained and evaluated for two trigger configurations: one based only on tracking and time-of-flight information, and one based only on electromagnetic-calorimeter information. Each classifier uses 48 binary Level-1 trigger conditions of the BESIII detector. The models distinguish hadron events from beam-background events and are evaluated against the established trigger selection at matched hadron-event efficiency. Relative to the established trigger selection, the beam-background false-positive rate is reduced by 20-60% across the studied configurations and center-of-mass energies, with stable suppression performance as tested using random-trigger data. Hardware-oriented synthesis indicates that the compact network is suitable for low-latency FPGA implementation. The proposed method can reduce the fraction of beam-background events in the trigger stream in future BESIII trigger upgrades. It also provides a reference for trigger optimization at future high-luminosity electron-positron colliders.

arXiv:2610.26188 §

Measurements of time-dependent b-hadron properties in LHCb

Lucas Foreman

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

These proceedings present the latest measurements of time-dependent properties of \(b\)-hadrons using the LHCb Run 2 data set. Three results are presented. The first of these is a measurement of the CKM phase \(\phi_s\) with \(B_s^0\to J/\psi\pi^+\pi^-\). This result improves the determination in this channel, obtaining a result of \(\phi_s = -77\pm34(\text{stat})\pm7(\text{syst})\) mrad. This result is included in the LHCb global average to obtain \(\phi_s = -41\pm17\) mrad. Additionally, a determination of the penguin contributions, \(\Delta\phi_s\), to measurements of \(\phi_s\) using \(B^0\to J/\psi\rho^0(770)\) is presented. This measurement constitutes the first observation of CP-violation in \(B_0 \to J/\psi\rho^0(770)\) decays. The CKM phase \(2\beta\) is measured and is determined to be \(2\beta^{\text{eff}}_{ccd} = 0.710\pm0.084\pm0.051\) rad. The most stringent constraint on penguin contributions is obtained and determined to be \(\Delta\phi_s = 5.0\pm4.6\) mrad. Finally, the first measurement of time-dependent CP-violation in a \(b\to s l^+ l^-\) process using the decay $B_s^0 \to K_S \mu^+ \mu^- $ is presented. The time-dependent coefficients of this decay are determined to be \(C=-0.13\pm0.32(\text{stat})\pm0.04(\text{syst})\) and \(S=+0.82\pm0.29(\text{stat})\pm0.05(\text{syst})\).

arXiv:2610.26343 §

Neural Bayesian Online Calibration with Deficient Simulators for MADMAX Dark Matter Experiment

Nabil Salama

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Scientific experiments often rely on deficient simulators and hardware whose commanded settings differ from the states realized in practice, limiting reliable optimization and inference. A Bayesian online calibration framework is introduced for experiments with at least one directly measurable simulator output and potentially latent quantities of scientific interest. Pre-calibration data are used to learn state-dependent effective simulator parameters, while a normalizing flow enables amortized posterior inference from measurement histories. During calibration, measurement and positioning uncertainties are propagated, and hardware commands are selected by balancing expected improvement with information gain. The framework is applied to the MADMAX dark matter experiment, where measured reflectivity is used to calibrate a deficient disk-booster simulator and infer the unobservable axion-signal power boost factor. Experimental validation on the MADMAX experiment is ongoing.

arXiv:2610.26673 §

Measurement of the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic amplitudes in J/ψ→Σ⁺ Σ̅⁻

BESIII Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Using 284 pb\(^{-1}\) of \(e^{+}e^{-}\) collision data collected with the BESIII detector at center-of-mass energies between 3.00 and 3.12 GeV, the relative phase between the strong and electromagnetic interaction amplitudes in the process \(J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-\) is determined. The relative phase is measured to be \((107.9 \pm 24.9)^{\circ}\) or \((-107.6 \pm 24.3)^{\circ}\), where the uncertainty includes both statistical and systematic sources. This is the first experimental determination of the strong-electromagnetic phase difference in \(J/\psi\) decays to a hyperon-antihyperon pair. The branching fraction \(\mathcal{B}_{J/\psi \to \Sigma^+ \bar{\Sigma}^-}\) is measured to be \((1.14 \pm 0.04)\times 10^{-3}\) for the positive phase solution or \((1.19 \pm 0.04) \times 10^{-3}\) for the negative phase solution. These results provide important input for testing perturbative QCD predictions and studies of SU(3) flavor symmetry.

arXiv:2610.22452 §

Observation of the Ξ_b⁻→Ξ⁻μ⁺μ⁻ and study of the Ξ_b⁻→Ξ⁻ψ(2S) decays

LHCb collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The rare decay \({\Xi_b^-\to\Xi^-\mu^+\mu^-}\) is observed for the first time using proton-proton collision data collected by the LHCb experiment at centre-of-mass energies of \(7\), \(8\) and~\(13~\text{TeV}\), corresponding to integrated luminosities of \(1\), \(2\) and \(6~\text{fb}^{-1}\), respectively. Its branching fraction is reported in intervals of the dimuon invariant mass squared. The integrated value of \({\cal{B}}(\Xi_b^-\to\Xi^-\mu^+\mu^-)\) is measured to be \({ (118 \pm 24 \pm 7 \pm 38 ) \times 10^{-8}}\), where the first uncertainty is statistical, the second is systematic and the third uncertainty arises from the normalisation branching fraction \({\cal{B}}(\Xi_b^-\to\Xi^-J/\psi)\), whose uncertainty is dominated by SU(3)-breaking effects. In addition, the ratio of the branching fractions of the \({\Xi_b^-\to\Xi^-\psi(2S)}\) and the \({\Xi_b^-\to\Xi^-J/\psi}\) decays is measured to be \({ 0.491 \pm 0.041 \pm 0.019 }\).

arXiv:2610.22504 §

A comparative study of variational quantum classifiers and classical machine learning for tW-tt̅ discrimination at the LHC

Rohan Hindocha et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Precise measurements of single top-quark production in the associated \(tW\)-channel require efficient discrimination against the dominant \(t\bar{t}\) background. In this work, we investigate the applicability of quantum machine learning to this classification problem using the Variational Quantum Classifier (VQC) algorithm. Simulated proton-proton collision events from CMS Open Data corresponding to Run-2 are employed to train and evaluate the VQC model. Its performance is compared with two established classical approaches, Boosted Decision Trees (BDTs) and Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP) using both machine learning metrics and physics-motivated metrics that describe the Signal Significance as a function of Signal Efficiency. The comparison is performed separately for the dileptonic \(e-\mu\) channel and for the resolved and boosted hadronic topologies. In addition, signal-enriched and background-enriched phase-space regions are studied to assess the dependence of classifier performance on event topology. Our results provide a systematic benchmark for the application of VQC for a realistic High Energy Physics classification task and identify the analysis regimes wherein quantum-inspired models offer competitive discrimination with respect to established classical techniques.

arXiv:2610.22785 §

Laboratory Characterization of a Ge-CCD Prototype for Future Near-Infrared Astronomical Instrumentation

Emi Tamura et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Germanium charge-coupled devices (Ge-CCDs) extend CCD imaging beyond the silicon cutoff and are candidate detectors for the near-infrared channel of future astronomical instruments, in particular the spectrographs of next-generation wide-field surveys. We report the laboratory characterization at Brookhaven National Laboratory (BNL) of a prototype fabricated at MIT Lincoln Laboratory. We describe the cryogenic test system and readout, and present measurements of the output waveform, dark current, readout noise, and X-ray response. The dark current at 94.69 K is \(26.12 \pm 0.08\) e\(^-\) pixel\(^{-1}\) s\(^{-1}\). Under \(^{55}\)Fe irradiation without on-chip binning, the charge generated by an X-ray is confined within \(2\times2\) pixels, and the Mn K\(\alpha\) line has a full width at half maximum (FWHM) of \(8.5 \pm 0.1\)% for single-pixel events. Since the charge generated by X-rays above about 30 keV spreads over \(3\times3\) or more pixels, the CCD was operated with \(2\times2\) on-chip binning at higher energies. This yielded FWHMs of \(8.4\pm0.4\)% at 30.9 keV (\(^{133}\)Ba, ASCA grades 0, 2, 3, 4, and 6) and \(7.45\pm0.04\)% at 59.5 keV (\(^{241}\)Am, all grades). These results establish a baseline for the device and for the BNL test system. Measurements of the temperature dependence, optical response, and charge-transfer performance will connect this baseline to the requirements of near-infrared astronomical instruments.

arXiv:2610.23112 §

A REST-based Monte Carlo Simulation and Data Analysis Framework for the NνDEx Experiment

Siyuan Huang et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The N\(\nu\)DEx experiment is designed to search for neutrinoless double-beta decay (\(0\nu\beta\beta\)) of \(\mathrm{^{82}Se}\) and is planned to be deployed at the China Jinping Underground Laboratory (CJPL). It employs a high-pressure time projection chamber (TPC) filled with \(\mathrm{^{82}SeF_6}\) gas and a Topmetal pixelated charge readout system. The high \(Q_{\beta\beta}\) value of \(\mathrm{^{82}Se}\), together with the track reconstruction capability of a gaseous TPC, provides strong potential for background suppression. In this work, we develop a full simulation and reconstruction framework for the N\(\nu\)DEx experiment based on the REST (Rare Event Searches Toolkit) software framework. To meet the detector-specific requirements of N\(\nu\)DEx, customized simulation processes are implemented to describe the detector geometry, electron drift and diffusion, the Topmetal readout plane, signal formation, and electronics response. These developments enable a complete software description of the detector response from energy deposition to digitized waveforms. A waveform-fitting-based reconstruction method is further applied to the simulated signals to extract the deposited charge and reconstruct the event energy. For simulated signal events, the reconstructed energy resolution reaches a full width at half maximum (FWHM) of less than 1\%, satisfying the design requirement of the experiment. The resulting simulation and reconstruction chain provides a basis for future studies of detector response, background rejection, event classification, and optimization of the N\(\nu\)DEx detector design.

arXiv:2610.23331 §

Reactor Antineutrino Detection with JUNO-TAO: Commissioning Performance and First Results

JUNO Collaboration

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The Taishan Antineutrino Observatory (TAO) is a satellite experiment of JUNO, designed to measure the reactor antineutrino energy spectrum with high precision at a baseline of 43.2~m from a reactor core using a 2.8-ton spherical liquid-scintillator detector, 1.8~m in diameter, operated at -50 degree and instrumented with 4,024 silicon photomultiplier tiles. The TAO detector installation was completed in 2025, and physics data taking began on 10 February 2026. This paper reports the detector design, calibration strategy, and performance based on commissioning data and the first physics runs. Stable cryogenic operation has been achieved, and about 90\% of the readout channels are retained for physics analysis after channel-quality selections. The detector light yield is measured to be about 4000~photoelectrons/MeV after correction for dark noise and optical crosstalk, using neutron capture on hydrogen in inverse beta decay (IBD) events. The energy resolution is about 2.8\% for \(^{68}\)Ge at the detector center and about 3.7\% for \(^{137}\)Cs at \(R=193\)~mm. This energy-resolution performance is among the best reported for reactor-antineutrino detectors and enables TAO to measure the reactor antineutrino spectrum with high precision. Within the fiducial volume of \(R < 800\)~mm, the vertex reconstruction bias remains within \(\pm 30~\mathrm{mm}\), and the resolution is approximately \(70~\mathrm{mm}\) over most of the fiducial volume. The combined muon-tagging efficiency of the veto system is about 97\%. Using 31.3 days of reactor-on data and 29.4 days of reactor-off data, a significant excess of IBD candidates is observed in the reactor-on sample relative to the reactor-off sample, with candidate rates of 2900\(\pm\)10 events/day and 419\(\pm\)4 events/day, respectively. The results demonstrate that TAO has reached a high performance level in reactor antineutrino measurement.

arXiv:2610.20756 §

Hyperon--nucleon interactions at BESIII

Hong-Fei Shen

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Hyperon–nucleon interactions provide essential information on the strange sector of baryon–baryon forces, with implications for hypernuclei, dense baryonic matter, and non-perturbative QCD. Direct experimental information, however, remains scarce because hyperons are short lived and intense conventional hyperon beams are difficult to produce. At BESIII, hyperons and antihyperons produced in charmonium decays can instead be used as incident particles, while the detector material serves as an effective fixed target. This approach has enabled a new program of hyperon–nucleon studies at an \(e^+e^-\) collider. Representative results include the first study of \(\Xi^{0}n\to\Xi^-p\) using \(\Xi^0\)–nucleus scattering, measurements of elastic \(\Lambda p\to\Lambda p\) and \(\bar{\Lambda}p\to\bar{\Lambda}p\) scattering, and recent observations of \(\bar{\Lambda}p\) annihilation into multi-meson final states. These measurements demonstrate that BESIII can operate as an effective hyperon–nucleon scattering facility and provide new experimental constraints on the poorly explored strange sector of baryon–baryon interactions.

First Observation of the Radiative Decay D⁰ →γω

BESIII Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

We report the first observation of the radiative charm decay \(D^0 \to \gamma \omega\) with a significance of \(6.0\sigma\). The analysis uses 20.3\,\(\rm{fb}^{-1}\) of \(e^+e^-\) collision data collected by the BESIII detector at a center-of-mass energy of 3.773\,GeV, and employs a deep learning strategy based on the Particle Transformer architecture. The absolute branching fraction of \(\mathcal{B}(D^0\to\gamma\omega)=(2.12\pm 0.40_{\rm stat}\pm 0.25_{\rm syst})\times 10^{-5}\) confirms the dominance of long-distance hadronic dynamics in radiative charm decays and provides a valuable benchmark for testing non-perturbative QCD calculations of \(D \to \gamma V\) transitions.

arXiv:2610.19668 §

Achieving a 0.49 keV threshold in the COSINE-100 dark matter experiment (J. Kim (COSINE-100 Collaboration) et al.)

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The COSINE-100 experiment searched for weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) with NaI(Tl) crystals at the Yangyang Underground Laboratory. Using its full 6-year dataset, we lower the analysis threshold to 0.49 keV. A multi-layer perceptron classifier separates scintillation events from photomultiplier tube noise. It is trained with approximately \(3\times10^{6}\) simulated scintillation waveforms per crystal, separately for groups of events with similar numbers of photoelectron clusters. The selection keeps more than 10% of scintillation events at 0.49 keV while limiting noise contamination to below 1%. The selection efficiency is validated with \(^{22}\)Na-calibration data and internal \(^{22}\)Na events, and the selection performance is stable over the six years of data taking. With this threshold, the median expected upper limit on the spin-dependent WIMP-proton cross section reaches \(1.1\times10^{-36}\) cm\(^2\) at a WIMP mass of 2.5 GeV/\(c^2\), probing previously unexplored parameter space in the 2.0-2.8 GeV/\(c^2\) mass range.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Using \(e^+e^-\) collision data collected by the BESIII detector at BEPCII corresponding to a total integrated luminosity of 44.6 \(\rm fb^{-1}\), we present the first measurement of the Born cross sections for the process \(e^+e^-\to pK^{-}\bar{\Sigma}^{0}\) + c.c. at fifty-six center-of-mass energies between 3.51 and 4.95~GeV. By fitting the dressed cross sections of \(e^+e^-\to pK^{-}\bar{\Sigma}^{0}\) + c.c. with the assumption of a power-law function plus a charmonium(-like) resonance, i.e., \(\psi(3770)\), \(\psi(4040)\), \(\psi(4160)\), \(Y(4230)\), \(Y(4360)\), \(\psi(4415)\), \(\psi(4500)\), \(Y(4660)\) or \(\psi(4710)\), no significant charmonium(-like) state decaying into \(pK^{-}\bar{\Sigma}^{0}\) + c.c. is observed. The product of branching fractions and electronic partial widths including their upper limits at the 90\% confidence level are provided for each decay. In addition, a partial wave analysis of the process \(e^+e^-\to pK^{-}\bar{\Sigma}^{0}\) + c.c. at \(\sqrt{s}\) = 3.773 GeV is performed to investigate \(N^{*}\), \(\Lambda^{*}\) and \(\Sigma^{*}\) resonances in the \(K^{-}\bar{\Sigma}^{0}\) and \(pK^{-}\) invariant mass spectra. arXiv:2610.20511 §

Measurement of electromagnetic transition form factors in two-photon collisions at BESIII (Max Lellmann (on behalf of the BESIII collaboration))

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The muon anomalous magnetic moment, \(a_\mu = (g-2)_\mu/2\), shows a sizeable discrepancy between Standard Model (SM) predictions and direct measurements, hinting at either New Physics or limitations in modeling low-energy strong interactions. As the uncertainty in the SM predictions currently exceeds the experimental uncertainty by a substantial margin, further improvements in theoretical precision are essential to clarify the origin of this discrepancy. The SM theoretical uncertainty is dominated by hadronic processes. The hadronic light-by-light (HLbL) scattering contribution relies on highly accurate data for hadron production via two-photon fusion. Operating at \(\sqrt{s}=3.773\,\text{GeV}\) with over \(20\,\text{fb}^{-1}\) of integrated luminosity, the BESIII experiment is ideally suited to probe these processes at small momentum transfers. By precisely measuring channels like \(e^+e^-\to e^+ e^-\pi^0\) and \(e^+e^-\to e^+ e^-\pi^0\pi^0\), BESIII provides the critical experimental input necessary to sharpen the SM prediction and clarify the true nature of the \(a_\mu\) anomaly.

arXiv:2610.14144 §

Planck-Scale Constraints on Quantum Gravity-Induced Neutrino Decoherence with IceCube

R. Abbasi et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Many quantum gravity theories predict spacetime fluctuations at the Planck scale. Neutrinos, which interact only via the weak force and gravity, propagate over long distances without losing quantum coherence. Planck-scale fluctuations would spoil that coherence, damping the flavor oscillation probability over long baselines. This makes neutrinos sensitive interferometers for Planck-scale physics. We present a search for quantum decoherence using 368,071 atmospheric neutrinos detected by IceCube in the energy range 0.5-100 TeV, an order of magnitude higher in energy than any previous decoherence search. We find no evidence for decoherence and set the strongest constraints to date on this class of neutrino decoherence models, improving on previous limits by over an order of magnitude for decoherence rate scaling as \(E^2\) and \(E^3\). These results exclude natural Planck-suppressed decoherence for all energy scalings \(E^n\) with \(0\leq n \leq 3\), the first such exclusion at the Planck scale for \(n=3\).

The differential branching fraction is determined across different regions of dimuon mass squared, \(q^2\), in the interval \(0.1 < q^2 < 18.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}\), as well as in different regions of $ m_{K\pi}$ in the interval \(1.1 < q^2 < 6.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}\). The results represent the first differential measurement of the branching fraction across \(m_{K\pi}\) within the selected dihadron-mass region, and the first observation of the decay within this region above the \(J/\psi\) resonance.

Energy-energy correlators within Z-tagged jets in proton-proton collisions at √(s) = 13 TeV

LHCb collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Energy-energy correlators probe perturbative and nonperturbative QCD regimes of jet formation through momentum-weighted angular correlations between hadrons. Forward decay chain reconstruction with displaced vertices in intense magnetic fields

Sergio Javier Jaimes Elles et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Precise reconstruction of particle decay chains is essential for a broad range of analyses in particle physics experiments, particularly those targeting flavour physics and spectroscopy. We present a standalone, modular and extensible decay tree reconstruction framework designed to handle complicated topologies, accounting for tightly constrained particle decays and long-distance trajectory extrapolations through regions of intense and inhomogeneous magnetic field, as well as limited momentum resolution. Implemented in C++, the framework is optimised for configurability and performance. Developed primarily within CERN’s LHCb experiment, its focus is on decay chains with vertices located several metres from the LHC proton-proton interaction point, where the dipole magnetic field is maximal and non-uniform. Even so, it remains flexible: it can accommodate these special cases as well as the approximations commonly used in standard decay tree fitting tools, and the geometries of general-purpose collider detectors. Benchmark studies using simulated data demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction efficiency, invariant mass resolution, and vertex position resolution with respect to existing tools, opening new opportunities for precision measurements and searches involving highly displaced decays.

Real-time Track Reconstruction with FPGAs: an example from MUonE

Michael McGinnis et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The feasibility of real-time track fitting of straight tracks for online event selection is shown in the context of the MUonE experiment. In order to reach the high throughput and low latency required for real-time track fitting, the tracking algorithm is implemented using High-Level Synthesis for an FPGA, which provides provides a balance between flexibility and performance. Hardware tracking results from a test with high-rate muons at the CERN M2 beam are presented. This test, a joint effort by the MUonE and Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) experiments, operated with Two-Strip (2S) modules provided by the CMS tracker group. The prototype version of the fitter used in this test achieves 3.75 \(\mu\)s latency and 7.75 % resource use. A comparison of the results with those obtained from a pure software implementation shows agreement well below relevant uncertainty scales. Developments since the test are also presented, with the hardware algorithm now reaching 1.35 \(\mu\)s latency and producing pipelined fitting results above 50 MHz while requiring less resources than the initial version. Doing so required including certain assumptions in the algorithm, but these assumptions hold for linear track fitting in general and algorithm remains useful in contexts other than MUonE.

arXiv:2610.15919 §

RINO: Renormalization Group Distillation with No Labels

Zichun Hao et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Supervised machine learning in high-energy physics (HEP) requires training on labeled simulations of particle collisions, which are known to be unfaithful to the underlying physics. This leads to significant inaccuracies and biases in the transfer of trained models to real data, limiting the power of deep learning methods. Self-supervised learning (SSL) is a promising alternative for pretraining directly on real, unlabeled data. However, existing techniques largely operate with inductive biases inspired by domains such as images and natural language. We propose RINO (Renormalization Group DIstillation with NO Labels), which, for the first time, exploits the fundamentally multi-scale renormalization group structure of high-energy jet formation as a physically grounded source of SSL views. We define teacher-student representations across energy scales using different levels of hierarchical clustering, aligned via DINO-style self-distillation. To mirror the background-dominated nature of real collider data, RINO pretrains exclusively on jets from quantum chromodynamics (QCD) processes and is evaluated on out-of-distribution (OOD) transfer to rare signal classes. In our controlled data-efficiency study, RINO matches fully supervised training on JetNet after finetuning on only 1% of the labeled data. With the full labeled dataset, RINO improves OOD classification accuracy on top-quark jets by 5.9% and, without retraining, improves AUC on the unseen Higgs boson decay channel \(H \to 4q\) by 4.1% over the strongest SSL baseline. Scale-structure diagnostics reveal that DINO-style self-distillation permits our backbone to retain scale-dependent structure. Ablation studies show that this accounts for an 8.6% OOD accuracy gap with respect to alternative SSL paradigms such as SimCLR. Code is available at https://github.com/zichunhao/RINO.

arXiv:2610.15963 §

Using Line Segment Tracking to enable ML-based Track Reconstruction in CMS

Aashay Arora on behalf of the CMS Collaboration

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

High-pileup conditions in CMS during the HL-LHC era make charged-particle tracking increasingly challenging as detector occupancy and combinatorics grow. We present a hybrid approach that exploits Line Segment Tracking (LST) objects rather than individual hits to enable the first CMS ML-based track reconstruction algorithm. The LST objects, namely the triplets used in this study, are built according to geometry- and physics-driven criteria and carry richer local structure, geometrical cues, and physics quantities such as transverse momentum, which helps the model incorporate detector-level information from the start. We apply a Transformer encoder with geometrically masked sparse self-attention to embed these objects in a learned latent space, and use object condensation to assemble full tracks in a single inference step. This object-based representation reduces complexity in dense environments and makes the model less sensitive to ambiguities introduced by high pileup. Evaluated on CMS Phase-2 simulation of \(t\bar{t}\) events at \(\langle \mu \rangle = 200\), the approach achieves reconstruction efficiency comparable to the LST quintuplet baseline while reducing the fake rate by roughly a factor of three and suppressing duplicates almost entirely, demonstrating the promise of advanced ML architectures for next-generation particle tracking.

A new AI-based Full Event Interpretation model for flavour physics

Matteo Barbieri et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The increasing event complexity and data rates foreseen for future LHC coinditions motivate a transition from selecting proton-proton (\(pp\)) collision events by reconstructing predefined signal candidates to constructing a reduced data stream by interpreting the relevant event features in a signal-agnostic way. We explore the capabilities of deep-learning-based architectures on heavy-flavour physics using LHCb as a case study. Experimental Demonstration of Soft-Tissue Muon Scattering Tomography

Dipanwita Banerjee et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Muons have fascinated physicists since their discovery in cosmic rays almost a century ago. Their large mass and relatively long lifetime allow relativistic muons to traverse large amounts of matter, a property that has been exploited to probe otherwise inaccessible structures, from volcanoes and archaeological monuments to shielded nuclear materials. Here, we experimentally demonstrate for the first time that muon scattering tomography can be extended to biological soft tissue. We image an anthropomorphic thorax phantom using a 2 GeV/c muon beam at the CERN Proton Synchrotron and reconstruct its three-dimensional scattering density from the measured deflections of individual muons. Our results extend muon imaging into a previously unexplored regime and open the possibility of using muons as a complementary probe for medical imaging.

Real-time monitoring of LHCb beam spot properties based on FPGA hit reconstruction

Giulio Cordova et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

arXiv:2610.12862 §

\textbf{\textsc{HepUKE}}: Uncertainty-aware Knowledge Evolution for High Energy Physics Analysis Agents

Zijie Shang et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Knowledge bases are essential to agents for high energy physics (HEP), supplying few-shot evidence for previously unseen tasks. Their principal utility lies in strategic guidance for novel analyses rather than ready-made answers or reusable code. Prevailing systems, however, treat all retrieved evidence as equally trustworthy and fail to accrue knowledge from the analyses under assistance. In this paper, inspired by the standard HEP treatment of uncertainty, we propose \textbf{\textsc{\underline{HepUKE}}}: an \textbf{\underline{U}}ncertainty-aware \textbf{\underline{K}}nowledge \textbf{\underline{E}}volution system for \textbf{\underline{HEP}} that repurposes information entropy to unify statistical uncertainty from LLM generation and systematic uncertainty from HEP methodological choices. Upon it, we construct a self-evolving domain-specific-language (DSL) knowledge base and release a large-scale expert-annotated HEP physics-analysis benchmark. Per-evidence confidence is quantified via the information entropy of sampled DSL generations: high-confidence precedents constrain program synthesis, whereas analyses lacking close precedents are validated and written back, sustaining continual knowledge evolution. To handle the highly redundant yet logically stringent nature of HEP knowledge, we introduce an early-stopping controller that fuses a backward stationarity test on the answer posterior with a forward marginal information gain estimate. On the 1,000-pair BESIII PhysicsQA benchmark, HepUKE reduces retrieval rounds by 43.0\% while preserving most answer quality, and on the out-of-domain HotpotQA benchmark, it reduces retrieval rounds by 14.5\% with a similar quality–efficiency trade-off. The code and datasets used in this work are available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/HepUKE-32FF.

arXiv:2610.13097 §

Observation of a Threshold Enhancement in the π⁰π⁰ Mass Spectrum in ψ(3686) →π⁰π⁰J/ψ Decays

BESIII Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Using a data sample of \((2712.4\pm14.4)\times 10^{6}\) \(\psi(3686)\) events collected with the BESIII detector, we report a high-precision study of the \(\pi^{0}\pi^{0}\) mass spectrum from \(\psi(3686)\rightarrow\pi^{0}\pi^{0}J/\psi\) decays. The enhancement previously observed near the \(\pi\pi\) mass threshold in the \(\pi^+\pi^-\) channel is confirmed, with a statistical significance exceeding 10\(\sigma\). A fit with a Breit-Wigner function yields a mass of \(M = 274.3\pm 2.1_{\rm stat.}\pm0.6_{\rm syst.}~{\rm MeV}/c^2\) and a width of \(\Gamma = 17.1\pm 1.5_{\rm stat.}\pm0.5_{\rm syst.}~{\rm MeV}\), consistent with those from the \(\pi^{+}\pi^{-}\) channel. By including final-state interactions, we analyze the data using both chiral perturbation theory (ChPT) and QCD multipole expansion (QCDME). While the ChPT model describes the higher-mass region above 0.3 GeV/\(c^2\) well, it fails to reproduce the significant threshold enhancement. In contrast, the QCDME model, which incorporates the \(S\)- and \(D\)-wave admixture structure of \(\psi(3686)\), provides a better description of the full \(\pi^{0}\pi^{0}\) mass spectrum.

We perform a simultaneous amplitude analysis of the decays \(D^0 \to K_{\text{S}}^{0}\pi^{+}\pi^{-}\) and \(D^0 \to K_{\text{L}}^{0}\pi^{+}\pi^{-}\) using an \(e^+e^-\) collision data sample, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of \(20.3\,\text{fb}^{-1}\), collected at the centre-of-mass energy of \(3.773\,\text{GeV}\) with the BESIII detector. Recent results in b →s ℓℓ transitions at LHCb

Juan Jose Juan Castella

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Rare decays of \(B\) hadrons, such as \(b\rightarrow s\ell \ell\) processes, provide great avenues to search for the influence of new physics (NP). They are highly suppressed in the Standard Model (SM) and therefore sensitive to NP contributions. In recent years, experimental measurements have shown tension with SM predictions. It is still up for debate whether these stem from genuine NP or an incomplete understanding of the theory. These proceedings focus on the recent efforts of the LHCb collaboration to advance this field. Some of the results covered show that tensions in these processes, like in \(B^0 \rightarrow K^{*0}\, \mu^+ \mu^-\), still persist upon higher precision. Others aim to shed some light on the theoretical picture through amplitude analyses of decays like \(B^0 \rightarrow K^{*0}\, \mu^+ \mu^-\) and \(B^+ \rightarrow K^+ \, \mu^+ \mu^-\). We also present two innovative measurements: the branching fraction of \(B^0 \rightarrow K^+ \pi^- \mu^+ \mu^-\) at large dihadron mass; and the first hadron collider measurement of the lepton flavour universality ratio of \(B^0 \rightarrow K^{*0} \, \ell^+\ell^-\) at high \(q^2\) (dilepton mass squared).

arXiv:2610.13634 §

A machine-learning method for the estimation of the background from jets misidentified as hadronic tau lepton decays

CMS Collaboration

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Backgrounds arising from quark- and gluon-initiated jets misidentified as hadronically decaying tau leptons constitute a major experimental challenge for analyses involving tau leptons. The MUltivariate Fake-Factor INference (MUFFIN) approach, a machine-learning-based method for estimating this background directly from observed events, is presented. Boosted decision tree reweighting is used to learn multidimensional, per-object correction factors directly from observed data, thereby modelling the jet to hadronic tau lepton misidentification probability across the kinematic and topological phase space without relying on low-dimensional parametrisations. Performance studies are presented using proton-proton collision data collected by the CMS experiment at the CERN LHC at \(\sqrt{s}\) = 13.6 TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 62.4 fb\(^{-1}\). Compared to low-dimensional parametrisation methods, improved modelling of key kinematic observables is achieved, particularly in regions of high \(p_\mathrm{T}\). The improved modelling leads to a reduction of up to 70% in the systematic uncertainty associated with jets being misidentified as hadronically decaying tau leptons. The MUFFIN method provides a robust and scalable method for background estimation in precision measurements and searches for beyond-the-standard-model physics involving tau lepton final states.

ATLAS Collaboration

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The first single-experiment observation of single top-quark production in the \(s\)-channel is presented. Measurements of this process are essential to fully characterize the electroweak interactions of the top quark and probe for new physics, however its highly suppressed cross-section has so far caused it to evade direct detection at the Large Hadron Collider. The analysis uses proton–proton collision data collected at a center-of-mass energy of \(\sqrt{s}=13\) TeV with the ATLAS detector, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of \(140\) fb\(^{-1}\). arXiv:2610.11782 §

Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND): Design and Initial Performance

SBND Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The Short-Baseline Near Detector (SBND) is a 112 tonne active mass liquid argon time projection chamber (LArTPC) situated in the Booster Neutrino Beam at Fermilab, forming part of the Short-Baseline Neutrino (SBN) Program. SBND began operation in 2024 and has since accumulated the world’s largest sample of neutrino-argon interactions. This paper describes the as-built detector and presents performance results from the first year of beam operation. All detector systems performed at or above design requirements. The liquid argon purity achieved an electron lifetime exceeding 30ms, ten times the design requirement, for more than 95% of Run 1. The TPC wire plane readout achieved noise levels at the theoretical minimum for the given wire geometry (an average of 388e\(^-\) for the 4m long collection plane wires) with 99.3% of readout channels live. Together, the liquid argon purity and TPC performance are enabling extremely clean event reconstruction in the SBND TPC. The photon detection system, consisting of passive reflectors and multiple active components (PMTs and X-ARAPUCAs), has achieved a light yield exceeding 10 photoelectrons per MeV throughout the detector volume and a timing resolution of a few nanoseconds, sufficient to resolve the 19ns bunch structure of the Booster Neutrino Beam. A nearly 4\(\pi\) cosmic ray tagger system surrounds the TPC to mitigate cosmic backgrounds in neutrino and BSM physics analyses. The data acquisition system has demonstrated stable performance up to an event rate of 8Hz, and a data collection efficiency of 98.6% was achieved throughout SBND’s first neutrino run in 2025. These results demonstrate that SBND represents the state of the art in multi-tonne-scale LArTPC detector performance.

Using Geometric Deep Learning for the Detection of the Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background in Water-Cherenkov Detectors

Matthieu Melennec et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The study of the Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background (DSNB) is an essential approach to improve our understanding of the formation of Core-Collapse Supernovae, as well as investigating fundamental neutrino properties. The detection of the DSNB flux however is a difficult task, as it requires the disentanglement of its signal with many competitive backgrounds in the low energy range. The main DSNB detection channel is the Inverse Beta Decay (IBD), which is characterised by a distinct, time-separated two-signal signature. This work introduces the use of Graph Neural Networks (GNNs) to handle critical detection and reconstruction tasks for the DSNB through the IBD channel. We test our models on two simulations in a Hyper-Kamiokande-like water Cherenkov detector geometry, and show that we obtain performances that are comparable to the state of the art.

Optimising Geometric Deep Learning for Varied Reconstruction Tasks in High Granularity Calorimeters

Matthieu Melennec et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

In the recent years, high energy physics discoveries have been driven by the increasing of luminosity and/or detector granularity. This evolution gives access to bigger statistics and data samples, but can make it hard to process the detector outputs with current methods and algorithms. Graph Neural Networks (GNNs), have been shown to be powerful tools to address these challenges. While GNNs can deal with the non-euclidean nature of the data extracted from these detectors, their deployment presents significant difficulties, mainly from the computational complexity of the algorithms they are based on. We propose to use the known geometries of the detectors to optimise the algorithms used for GNN pipelines, which usually have quadratic algorithmic complexities. We validate the validity of our optimised approach on various tasks, such as particle identification, energy regression and instance segmentation. Finally, we present HIBOU, a generic modular architecture that provides a blueprint for how these GNN blocks are to be implemented within experiment-specific reconstruction frameworks.

arXiv:2610.12408 §

A compact, interrogable Tree Tensor Network Jet Tagger on Lund declustering trees

Fabrizio Napolitano et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Jet taggers built on the Lund declustering tree encode QCD radiation structure in the inputs and process it with graph networks. We ask whether the tree itself can also constrain the computation, and what becomes accessible when it does. For this, we construct a novel quantum-inspired Tree Tensor Network (TTN), where a single tensor core is shared among the internal nodes of the Cambridge/Aachen declustering tree, employing Lund coordinates, and with no particle-identification or detector-level information. The construction is based on a strong prior on the radiation history rather than on the constituents, complementary to compact equivariant taggers. Because the same learned map acts identically at every node, the trained model can be evaluated on truncated trees and its latent space compared across recursions, and used to localise where on the Lund jet plane the Pythia and Herwig jet representations diverge, showing the regions of generator-dependent concentration. The \(\chi=8\) model has 4014 trainable parameters against approximately 391,000 for LundNet. We evaluate boosted top tagging and quark/gluon discrimination at particle level on Pythia and Herwig samples, in both native (Pythia\(\to\)Pythia) and transfer (Pythia\(\to\)Herwig) configurations. Despite differing in capacity by two orders of magnitude, the TTN reaches native performance close to LundNet in quark/gluon discrimination, slightly underperforming in top tagging, with a similar Pythia-to-Herwig transfer behavior in both tasks. Finally, we show the behavior of the network as a function of the bond dimension, and as a function of the perturbative scale.

Search for rare charm decays at BESIII

Xiaokang Li et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The BESIII experiment searches for rare and symmetry-violating decays with the largest charmonium and threshold open-charm data samples. This contribution reviews recent searches for charmonium weak decays, charm flavour-changing neutral currents, charged-lepton flavour violation, and Bose-symmetry violation. arXiv:2610.08054 §

HybridMC: a fast detailed simulation framework for optical calorimeter technologies at the LHCb ECAL Upgrade II

Marco Pizzichemi et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The detailed Monte Carlo simulation of optical calorimeters is dominated by the generation and tracking of scintillation and Cherenkov photons, which in standard Geant4 ray-tracing increases the CPU time per electromagnetic shower by up to three orders of magnitude compared to simulating only the energy deposition. At the luminosity and granularity foreseen for the LHCb ECAL Upgrade II, this cost becomes prohibitive for both detector R&D and large-scale sample production. The optical photons cannot simply be discarded, because the time resolution of a few tens of picoseconds targeted by the upgrade to mitigate pile-up is driven by their arrival-time distribution at the photo-detectors. This paper presents HybridMC, a fast and detailed simulation framework for optical calorimeters that decouples shower development from optical transport. Energy deposition is handled by a standard Geant4 simulation without optical photon propagation, while light transport is reproduced by a parametrised summary Monte Carlo step based on probability density functions extracted from a one-time full ray-tracing optical calibration. A Monte Carlo-to-Monte Carlo validation shows good statistical agreement with full ray-tracing for all module types of the upgraded LHCb ECAL, from individual photon arrival times up to the reconstructed energy and time resolutions. The CPU time is reduced by more than two orders of magnitude, with module-dependent acceleration factors from 150 to 480. A comparison to test-beam data from DESY II and the CERN SPS shows that the framework reproduces the momentum dependence of the measured energy and time resolutions of a real prototype, up to a small residual constant term. HybridMC retains the microscopic optical detail not accessible to flash simulators based on generative models, at a CPU cost compatible with large-scale production on standard computing infrastructure.

arXiv:2610.04397 §

Simultaneous measurement of the Higgs boson decay rates to WW and ZZ in fully hadronic final states at FCC-ee

Jan Eysermans et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The precise determination of the Higgs boson couplings to electroweak gauge bosons is a key objective of the FCC-ee physics program. In particular, measurements of the Higgs boson couplings to W and Z bosons play a central role in the model-independent determination of its total decay width. This paper presents a study of Higgs boson decays to H -> WW and H -> ZZ in the Higgsstrahlung production process. The analysis focuses on events in which the associated Z boson and the vector bosons from the Higgs decay all decay hadronically, resulting in six-jet final states. The large H -> WW branching fraction leads to high signal statistics in the fully hadronic channel, while for H -> ZZ the much smaller Higgs branching fraction is partly compensated by the large hadronic branching fractions of the Z bosons. Due to the substantial kinematic overlap between the WW and ZZ decay modes, their signal strengths are extracted simultaneously. Jet-clustering and dedicated jet-pairing techniques are employed to optimize the reconstruction of the hadronically decaying gauge bosons and enhance the separation of the two signal components. Assuming a center-of-mass energy of 240 GeV and an integrated luminosity of 10.8 ab^-1, expected relative uncertainties of 1.48% and 8.20% are obtained on the production cross section times branching fraction, sigma(ZH) x BR, for the H -> WW and H -> ZZ decay modes, respectively.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

A search for beyond the standard model effects in high-mass dielectron and dimuon final states with the CMS detector is performed, with a specific focus on Higgs boson compositeness. Compositeness would manifest as distortions in the observed dilepton mass spectrum and the presence of bound states, such as the Higgs boson and new massive vector boson resonances. More stringent compositeness limits at the TeV scale are set in several new physics scenarios under the assumption that a new Z’ resonance is physically present within or slightly above the visible dilepton mass spectrum. In this context, cases with large resonance decay widths are explored for the first time.

Precision measurement of ocean tides using cosmic-ray muons

Jiwon Seo et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Cosmic-ray muons provide a natural probe of the integrated mass along their trajectories. Here we show that cosmic-ray muons can monitor ocean-tide-induced variations in seawater overburden within an underwater tunnel. The observed modulation closely tracks independent tide-gauge measurements, yielding an attenuation response coefficient of \(k=43.2\pm1.0\)~counts\(\rm /h/m\) and resolving meter-scale variations in seawater overburden. Unlike conventional tide gauges that measure sea level locally, muography measures the integrated overburden above the detector. These results demonstrate that muography can quantitatively resolve small, time-dependent variations in environmental overburden, providing a new approach to monitoring dynamic natural systems.

A graph neural network for deep learning-based full event interpretation at FCC-ee

Elena Graverini et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

This paper presents a deep learning-based full event interpretation prototype developed for the electron-positron Future Circular Collider (FCC-\(ee\)) using simulated \(e^{+}e^{-}\rightarrow Z\rightarrow b\bar{b}\) events at \(\sqrt{s} = 91 \ \mathrm{GeV}\). arXiv:2610.06117 §

SysVar: Consistent and scalable treatment of systematic uncertainties in template-based analyses

Ilias Tsaklidis et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Propagating systematic uncertainties from correction weights into templates generated from simulated data while preserving correlations in the signal extraction variables becomes increasingly challenging as analyses scale in size. SysVar’s lightweight API hides most of the bookkeeping complexity of applying correction weights, building systematic variations, and histogramming nominal and varied templates. This streamlines workflows that are otherwise error-prone but essential for high-precision measurements. Correlations are preserved by propagating the full covariance across the analysis and compressing it into a small set of orthogonal eigenvariations, each defining an ordinary shape nuisance parameter. In this work, we demonstrate SysVar’s workflow and impact by combining two independent pseudo-measurements that benefit from consistently correlated systematic uncertainties encoded in the template shapes. SysVar is open-source, pip-installable, and experiment-agnostic.

arXiv:2610.06379 §

FPGA-based muon shower identification and graph neural network tracking algorithms for HL-LHC triggers

Daniel Estrada et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

This work presents two hardware-accelerated strategies for the upgrade of the CMS Level-1 Trigger (L1T) to target efficiency losses due to highly energetic radiating muons and unconventional physics signatures, such as Long-Lived Particles (LLPs). Operating directly on low-level detector hits, the first strategy implements a dedicated muon shower identification algorithm in the CMS barrel region that monitors hit multiplicities in the Drift Tube chambers to generate Shower Primitives. Executing with sub-bunch-crossing latency and ultra-low resource consumption, this algorithm successfully tags showered muons and provides essential context for downstream track finders. The second approach targets the barrel-endcap transition (overlap) region by evaluating Graph Neural Networks to reconstruct displaced muon tracks. Following a divided methodology that first isolates hardware feasibility from evolving physics refinements, an initial proxy model based on a GraphSAGE architecture is evaluated to validate its viability on FPGAs within strict latency limits. This implementation utilizes an INT8-PO2 quantization technique coupled with a data-driven bit-width optimization. By replacing DSP-heavy fixed-point multiplications with fast compile-time arithmetic bit-shifts and allocating the minimum required bits per signal, this hardware-software co-design reduces DSP utilization to \(20\%\) and achieves a deterministic inference latency of just \(19\) clock cycles. Together, these developments pave the way for triggering on non-standard muon signatures, such as those from LLP decays, at L1T.

arXiv:2610.06676 §

Design optimization of the inter-electrode structures in DC-RSD

N. Cartiglia et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

C-coupled Resistive Silicon Detectors (RSD) share charge among electrodes through a resistive n\(^+\) surface layer, and the choice of inter-electrode structure — a trench, a resistive strip, or no structure at all — directly determines the achievable position resolution and its uniformity. This paper addresses the question of which structure best optimises the spatial resolution. To answer it quantitatively, DCRSD_SurfaceSimulator was developed, a ROOT-based tool that models the resistive surface as a two-dimensional Kirchhoff resistive network and solves for charge sharing as a function of hit position, surface resistivity, and inter-electrode structure. The simulator is validated against test-beam data from FBK DC-RSD sensors with 300, 500, and 1000~\(\mu\)m pixel pitch, reproducing the position resolution, its spatial uniformity, and the signal-sharing templates correctly. Using the validated simulator, trenches, resistive strips, and no containment are then compared on position resolution and its spatial uniformity, inter-electrode resistance, and signal leakage to outer pixels. Resistive strips are found to outperform trenches in resolution by approximately 25\% for all tested strip resistances (500–2000~\(\Omega\)) and input impedances (0–100~\(\Omega\)), at a surface resistivity of 1000~\(\Omega/\square\), and trace the origin of this difference to the spatial gradient of the charge-sharing fraction: trenches produce a markedly weaker and less uniform gradient, particularly away from the pixel centre, than either a resistive strip or a bare resistive surface.

arXiv:2610.06784 §

How to scale your HEP ML models: A recipe for robust architecture comparisons at scale

Matthias Vigl et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Much of the recent progress in machine learning domains such as language models has come from scaling laws that predict performance as a function of training effort. In high-energy physics (HEP) similar behavior has now been observed. To aid further study, we present a systematic procedure to derive robust scaling laws and compare design choices on the relevant budget axes for HEP tasks. We first validate the full scaling trajectory on toy problems and then apply the procedure to multi-task transformers on the ~11 billion-jet ATLAS JetSet2 dataset, in both the compute- and data-constrained regimes. For the latter, we predict, to the best of our knowledge for the first time, the jointly optimal model size, training horizon, learning rate and batch size under early stopping. At compute-optimal scaling, we recover a near-equal \(\sqrt{C}\) dependence of model and dataset size, and find that auxiliary objectives lower the primary jet-classification loss at equal compute budget. Expanding the inputs toward lower-level data systematically lowers the loss while leaving the scaling exponent nearly unchanged. The onset of the power-law regime is itself set by scale: below a threshold in dataset size the loss carries little information about high-compute scaling, underscoring the value of large, high-quality full-simulation datasets as a foundation for scaling studies and the development of foundation models in HEP.

arXiv:2610.06816 §

Reconfigurable On-Chip AI for Particle Physics Detectors with Logic Neural Networks

Mila Bileska et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

On-device machine learning is increasingly important in applications where extreme data rates or resource constraints make centralized processing infeasible. Logic neural networks (LNNs), which directly learn Boolean logic instead of conventional arithmetic operations, have recently emerged as a state-of-the-art approach for efficient inference and are particularly well suited for deployment in custom silicon. However, hard-wired solutions are typically optimized for narrowly defined tasks and lack the ability to adapt to changing data distributions. Next-generation pixel detectors in particle physics present a particularly challenging use case: their high granularity and readout frequency generate data rates that cannot be centrally processed, while stringent space and power constraints necessitate highly efficient on-chip inference. Moreover, radiation damage progressively alters detector response, requiring models that can be re-trained and reconfigured after deployment. In this work, we evaluate LNNs for on-chip inference in pixel detectors. We show that their superior resource efficiency may enable a step change in model capability, making more complex architectures feasible within the available hardware budget. LNNs also provide increased robustness against radiation-induced random bit flips. Finally, we present novel work on reconfigurable LNNs, enabling models to adapt to changing operating conditions after deployment.

First measurement of the low-momentum negative-muon beam for the muon-to-electron conversion experiment at J-PARC

The COMET Collaboration et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

The COMET Phase-\(\alpha\) experiment, the initial beam-operation stage of the COMET muon-to-electron conversion programme at J-PARC, took place in 2023 and marked the first beam operation at the COMET facility. It served to commission the delivery of a bunched, slow-extracted 8 GeV proton beam and the muon beamline. Its objective was to demonstrate experimentally that low-momentum negative muons could be transported to the experimental area through the Transport Solenoid (TS). We report a measurement of the transported muon beam using the Range Counter, a dedicated detector that identifies stopped negative muons by detecting delayed electrons emitted after the muons stop in a copper absorber. From the measured decay-time spectra, we reconstruct the number of stopped muons, the corresponding momentum spectrum, and the transverse beam profile. The observation of the stopped-muon component demonstrates that slow negative muons were transported through the TS to the experimental area. The reconstructed momentum spectrum indicates that the transported beam is concentrated in the low-momentum region. The non-uniform transverse profile is qualitatively consistent with charge drift in the curved solenoidal magnetic field. A Geant4-based simulation reproduces the overall dependence on degrader thickness and detector position; the measured yields are systematically higher by roughly 30%. These results provide the first experimental benchmark of low-momentum negative-muon transport in COMET and establish the basis for future beam characterisation and production studies.

Entanglement-sensitive observables in e⁺e⁻ →τ⁺τ⁻ at STCF: a detector-level feasibility study

Chentao Bao et al.

New hep-ex announcement — original abstract; no AI summary.

Quantum entanglement in \(\tau^+\tau^-\) production provides a direct probe of non-classical spin correlations in a relativistic quantum system, with the \(\tau\) decay products serving as spin analyzers. We investigate the detector-level feasibility of such measurements in \(e^+e^-\to\tau^+\tau^-\) at the Super Tau-Charm Facility (STCF) at \(\sqrt{s}=7\) GeV, using the \(\rho\rho\) and \(\pi\rho\) decay channels. These results demonstrate the detector-level feasibility of probing quantum entanglement and non-classical spin correlations in hadronic \(\tau\) decays at STCF.
